## Assistant · 2026-04-26T22:18:12

Most of the pieces are already there — what's missing is wiring them into a single automated harness. Here's what I'd propose, want your sign-off before building:

**Approach**: a new `tests/e2e_staging.rs` (or `xtask` binary) that runs end-to-end against the deployed gateway hitting Hedix staging, in three phases:

1. **Market review** — call `GET /admin/intel/discovery` to list markets + tradability, dump to `target/e2e/markets-{ts}.json`. Pick a small representative sample (a few tradeable + a couple intentionally bad to exercise rejection paths).
2. **RFQ exercise** — for each sample market, POST to `/api/v1/rfq/quote` with parametrized sides/qtys. Capture each request/response pair (metadata: latency, status, headers; payload: full JSON body) into `target/e2e/rfq-{ts}.jsonl`.
3. **Both-sides correlation** — after the run, fetch `/admin/observability/interactions` (the existing 256-entry `RingSink` of outbound Hedix calls), `/admin/observability/fills`, `/admin/observability/rejections`, `/admin/observability/fv-history`. Join on `quote_id`/timestamps and emit a single report (`target/e2e/report-{ts}.md`) with: per-RFQ inbound payload, our priced response, the outbound Hedix calls it triggered, the FV snapshot used, and the +60s drift once it's back-filled (so the run needs a ~70s soak before pulling drift).

**Main tradeoff**: this exercises the *deployed* gateway via its admin endpoints — accurate but requires the rings be wired to admin routes (some already are, some may not be) and the harness needs an admin token. Alternative is in-process (`#[tokio::test]` spinning the app inside the test binary against real Hedix), which is hermetic but doesn't exercise the actual Fly deploy.

A few decisions before I write code:
- **Where does it run?** Local-only (`cargo test --ignored e2e`), nightly CI against Fly, or both?
- **Inbound payload capture**: the rings don't store full `CreateQuoteRequest` JSON today — should I (a) have the harness log its own outbound payloads (simpler, complete) or (b) add a bounded inbound-request ring (more invasive, but useful beyond this test)?
- **"New prices"**: did you mean *validate the existing strategies' prices* on these markets, or are you about to land a new pricing model and want the harness ready to A/B it?
- **Write side**: memory says `HEDIX_WRITE_ENABLED=0` and `POST /api/mm/orders` 404s on staging — keep the harness read-only (quote+log, no fan-out), or attempt writes and assert the 404 path?

Tell me which lane you want and I'll build it.

Two important findings that change scope before I start coding:

**1. "Attempt write" — the gateway doesn't fan out to Hedix on quote accept.** Per `src/routes/quote.rs:29-34`, Hedix MM creates the counter-order from *our RFQ response body*. So "attempt write" could mean:
   - **(a)** Just run RFQs with `auto_accept=true` (already a real fill commitment from our side)
   - **(b)** Also probe `create_intents` directly from the harness as a separate test, to confirm the documented 404 — or maybe it now works
   - **(c)** Flip `HEDIX_WRITE_ENABLED=1` on the Fly app for the run window and see what the gateway does
   
   I'd default to (a)+(b): RFQs are the real e2e, and a small `create_intents` probe documents the write-endpoint state for free. Confirm?

**2. Auth is one X-API-Key** for both `/api/v1/rfq/*` and `/admin/*` (no separate admin token), and the `InteractionSink` (outbound Hedix calls) is not yet exposed via admin. I'll add `GET /admin/interactions` as part of the work.

**Proposed split** (3 PRs, in order):

| PR | What | Why separate |
|----|------|--------------|
| 1 | `InboundRequestRing` + `GET /admin/inbound` + env `RFQ_INBOUND_REQUEST_CAPACITY` | Self-contained observability addition; useful even without the harness; needs to be deployed before the harness can read it |

Optional `create_intents` probe at the end. | The harness needs PRs 1+2 deployed to Fly to function |

Each PR is reviewable on its own; PR 3 is gated on PRs 1+2 being live on `artelonga-rfq-gateway.fly.dev`.

## Assistant · 2026-04-26T23:31:29

Important find — `src/backtest/mod.rs:8` says: **"Phase 4.1 will load real `Interaction` records from disk once inbound-RFQ recording lands (pipeline-trace §6 gap #1)."** The PR1 we just shipped is the unblocker for that. The pieces for PnL are mostly already in the repo.

**Before e2e tests:** only PR2 (`InteractionSink` → `/admin/interactions`, ~30 LOC). Then PR3 (the harness) is unblocked.

**Where the answer to your bigger question lives — already in code:**

| Need | What exists | What's missing |
|------|-------------|----------------|
| Accurate prices | `intel::pricing_as::compute_as_quote` (Avellaneda-Stoikov core), Polymarket REST + WS feeds for FV anchor, A-S kill switches (VPIN, divergence, catalyst, fingerprint) | Tuning the `RFQ_AS_*` params against real data — needs the harness output |
| Quote quality review | `FillObservation.valuation` (FV at fill) + `mid_at_plus_60s_cents` (FV +60s) = realized adverse selection per fill; rejection ring with `code`/`reason` for accept-rate breakdown | A derived aggregator that joins them into a scorecard (mean edge captured, hit rate, adverse drift by superset/side/qty bucket). One admin endpoint, ~150 LOC |
| Paper-trade PnL | `backtest::run()` already computes per-scenario PnL given `settlement_cents`; A-S core is pure-functional so re-playable | (a) Real-data adapter: `InboundRequestObservation` + `FillObservation` → `Scenario`. (b) Settlement proxy for non-expired markets — use `mid_at_plus_60s_cents` as a short-horizon settlement anchor, since most staging markets won't actually expire during a run |

**Recommended sequence after PR2/PR3:**

1. **PR4 — Quote-quality scorecard** (`/admin/quote-quality`). Pure derived view over the existing rings. No new data capture, no risk. Surfaces: accept rate, mean edge captured (`valuation.fair_value_cents - our_price_cents`, sign-aware), mean +60s adverse drift, breakdown by superset / side / quantity bucket / rejection reason. This is the "quote quality" answer.
2. **PR5 — Real-data backtest adapter**. Harness pulls rings → builds `Scenario`s → feeds `backtest::run` with the live `AsParams`. Settlement proxy = `mid_at_plus_60s_cents` (or a longer horizon if you add T+5min back-fill). Output: paper PnL number per param sweep. Lets you A/B `RFQ_AS_*` knobs against actual traffic.
3. **PR6 — Param sweep CLI**. Run PR5 across a grid of `(γ, σ₀, k̂, θ_*)` values, pick the Pareto front of (paper PnL, accept rate). No live trading risk because A-S is a pure function over recorded inputs.

**The main tradeoff to flag:** Paper PnL on staging is *short-horizon* PnL (T+60s drift as settlement proxy), not terminal PnL — it captures whether you got picked off but not whether you guessed the event right. That's the right thing to optimize for an MM (we don't take directional risk, we collect the spread), but it'll under-count tail outcomes from inventory we held. Fine for v1; revisit when we have markets that actually settle in a run window.

**Step zero, though:** before touching strategy params, the e2e harness has to actually run end-to-end and produce a baseline report on `artelonga-rfq-gateway.fly.dev` with the current `RFQ_STRATEGY` (whatever's deployed now). Without a baseline number you can't tell if a change helped. So: PR2 → PR3 → first baseline run → THEN PR4/5/6.

Want me to start PR2 now?

1. `git push origin main`
2. `fly deploy`
3. Run the harness — first baseline report
4. Inspect → decide whether to start PR4 (quote-quality scorecard) or tune the live `RFQ_AS_*` params first

# Schedule Remote Agents

You are helping the user schedule, update, list, or run **remote** Claude Code agents. These are NOT local cron jobs — each routine spawns a fully isolated remote session (CCR) in Anthropic's cloud infrastructure, either on a recurring cron schedule or once at a specific time. The agent runs in a sandboxed environment with its own git checkout, tools, and optional MCP connections.

## First Step

The user has already told you what they want (see User Request at the bottom). Skip the initial question and go directly to the matching workflow.

## Setup Notes

⚠ Heads-up:

- No MCP connectors — connect at https://claude.ai/customize/connectors if needed.


## What You Can Do

Use the `RemoteTrigger` tool (load it first with `ToolSearch select:RemoteTrigger`; auth is handled in-process — do not use curl):

- `{action: "list"}` — list all routines
- `{action: "get", trigger_id: "..."}` — fetch one routine
- `{action: "create", body: {...}}` — create a routine
- `{action: "update", trigger_id: "...", body: {...}}` — partial update
- `{action: "run", trigger_id: "..."}` — run a routine now

(Note: the API uses `trigger_id` as the parameter name, but the user-facing term is "routine".)

You CANNOT delete routines. If the user asks to delete, direct them to: https://claude.ai/code/routines

## Create body shape

For a recurring schedule:

```json
{
  "name": "AGENT_NAME",
  "cron_expression": "CRON_EXPR",
  "enabled": true,
  "job_config": {
    "ccr": {
      "environment_id": "ENVIRONMENT_ID",
      "session_context": {
        "model": "claude-sonnet-4-6",
        "sources": [
          {"git_repository": {"url": "https://github.com/artelonga/rfq"}}
        ],
        "allowed_tools": ["Bash", "Read", "Write", "Edit", "Glob", "Grep"]
      },
      "events": [
        {"data": {
          "uuid": "<lowercase v4 uuid>",
          "session_id": "",
          "type": "user",
          "parent_tool_use_id": null,
          "message": {"content": "PROMPT_HERE", "role": "user"}
        }}
      ]
    }
  }
}
```

For a one-time run, replace `"cron_expression": "CRON_EXPR"` with `"run_once_at": "YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ"` (RFC3339 UTC, must be in the future). Everything else is identical.

Generate a fresh lowercase UUID for `events[].data.uuid` yourself.

## API Field Reference

### Create Routine — Required Fields
- `name` (string) — A descriptive name
- Exactly ONE of:
  - `cron_expression` (string) — 5-field cron in UTC. **Minimum interval is 1 hour.**
  - `run_once_at` (string) — RFC3339 UTC timestamp. Must be in the future. Fires once, then auto-disables.
- `job_config` (object) — Session configuration (see structure above)

### Create Routine — Optional Fields
- `enabled` (boolean, default: true)
- `mcp_connections` (array) — MCP servers to attach:
  ```json
  [{"connector_uuid": "uuid", "name": "server-name", "url": "https://..."}]
  ```

### Update Routine — Optional Fields
All fields optional (partial update):
- `name`, `cron_expression`, `run_once_at`, `enabled`, `job_config`
- `mcp_connections` — Replace MCP connections
- `clear_mcp_connections` (boolean) — Remove all MCP connections

### Cron Expression Examples

The user's local timezone is **America/Sao_Paulo**. Cron expressions and `run_once_at` timestamps are always in UTC. When the user says a local time, convert it to UTC but confirm with them: "9am America/Sao_Paulo = Xam UTC, so the cron would be `0 X * * 1-5`." For one-time runs, the same conversion applies — "run this at 3pm" → `"run_once_at": "YYYY-MM-DDTHH:00:00Z"` with their 3pm converted to UTC.

- `0 9 * * 1-5` — Every weekday at 9am **UTC**
- `0 */2 * * *` — Every 2 hours
- `0 0 * * *` — Daily at midnight **UTC**
- `30 14 * * 1` — Every Monday at 2:30pm **UTC**
- `0 8 1 * *` — First of every month at 8am **UTC**

Minimum interval is 1 hour. `*/30 * * * *` will be rejected.

### Current Time (for one-off runs)

When /schedule was invoked it was **Sun, Apr 26, 2026 at 09:33 PM** (America/Sao_Paulo) / **2026-04-27T00:33:55.714Z** UTC. Treat this as an approximate anchor only — the conversation may have been running for a while since then.

**Before computing any `run_once_at` value, you MUST re-check the current time** by running `date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ` via the Bash tool. Do not guess or infer today's date from conversation context. Resolve relative requests ("tomorrow at 9am", "in 3 hours", "next Monday") against the freshly fetched time, then echo the resolved local time AND the UTC timestamp back to the user for confirmation before creating the routine. If the resolved time is already in the past, ask the user to clarify rather than silently rolling forward.

## Workflow

### CREATE a new routine:

1. **Understand the goal** — Ask what they want the remote agent to do. What repo(s)? What task? Remind them that the agent runs remotely — it won't have access to their local machine, local files, or local environment variables.
2. **Craft the prompt** — Help them write an effective agent prompt. Good prompts are:
   - Specific about what to do and what success looks like
   - Clear about which files/areas to focus on
   - Explicit about what actions to take (open PRs, commit, just analyze, etc.)
3. **Set the schedule** — Ask when and how often. The user's timezone is America/Sao_Paulo. When they say a time (e.g., "every morning at 9am"), assume they mean their local time and convert to UTC for the cron expression. Always confirm the conversion: "9am America/Sao_Paulo = Xam UTC." If they want a one-time run (e.g., "once at 3pm", "tomorrow morning", "remind me to check X later"), use `run_once_at` instead of `cron_expression` — same timezone conversion applies. **First re-check the current time with `date -u` via Bash** (the reference time above may be stale in a long conversation), resolve the relative phrase against that fresh value, and confirm the resulting absolute timestamp with the user.
4. **Choose the model** — Default to `claude-sonnet-4-6`. Tell the user which model you're defaulting to and ask if they want a different one.
5. **Validate connections** — Infer what services the agent will need from the user's description. For example, if they say "check Datadog and Slack me errors," the agent needs both Datadog and Slack MCP connectors. Cross-reference with the connectors list above. If any are missing, warn the user and link them to https://claude.ai/customize/connectors to connect first. The default git repo is already set to `https://github.com/artelonga/rfq`. Ask the user if this is the right repo or if they need a different one.
6. **Review and confirm** — Show the full configuration before creating. Let them adjust.
7. **Create it** — Call `RemoteTrigger` with `action: "create"` and show the result. The response includes the routine ID. Always output a link at the end: `https://claude.ai/code/routines/{ROUTINE_ID}`

### UPDATE a routine:

1. List routines first so they can pick one
2. Ask what they want to change
3. Show current vs proposed value
4. Confirm and update

### LIST routines:

1. Fetch and display in a readable format
2. Show: name, schedule (human-readable), enabled/disabled, next run, repo(s)

### RUN NOW:

1. List routines if they haven't specified which one
2. Confirm which routine
3. Execute and confirm

## Important Notes

- These are REMOTE agents — they run in Anthropic's cloud, not on the user's machine. They cannot access local files, local services, or local environment variables.
- Always convert cron to human-readable when displaying
- When listing routines, `ended_reason: "run_once_fired"` means a one-shot already ran (shows as "Ran" in the web UI). The user can re-arm it by updating with a new `run_once_at`.
- Default to `enabled: true` unless user says otherwise
- Accept GitHub URLs in any format (https://github.com/org/repo, org/repo, etc.) and normalize to the full HTTPS URL (without .git suffix)
- The prompt is the most important part — spend time getting it right. The remote agent starts with zero context, so the prompt must be self-contained.
- To delete a routine, direct users to https://claude.ai/code/routines
- If the user's request seems to require GitHub repo access (e.g. cloning a repo, opening PRs, reading code), remind them that they should run /web-setup to connect their GitHub account (or install the Claude GitHub App on the repo as an alternative) — otherwise the remote agent won't be able to access it.

- **Schedule timing isn't tunable** — Fly's `--schedule weekly` runs ~7 days from machine creation, no specific day/time. If you want Monday 09:00 SP specifically, the simplest fix is `--schedule daily` + a day-of-week guard at the top of `e2e-harness/main.rs`. ~10 LOC. Want me to add it?
- **Log volume**: the canary dumps the full Markdown report (~150 lines) to `fly logs` once a week. Noisy but searchable; grep for `"RFQ Gateway E2E Run"`.
- **Image staleness**: the canary machine was launched with the *current* gateway image at creation time. If you redeploy the gateway, the canary keeps using the old image until you destroy/recreate it. To keep them in sync, either redeploy the canary on each gateway deploy or live with the drift. For a weekly cadence this is fine; can revisit if needed.

## User · 2026-04-27T01:22:21

next steps to prepare for user offers, cover all scenarios from  conservtive, risk averse to a continuum towards accept all

## Assistant · 2026-04-27T01:23:28

Big-picture question — let me think about what "the knob" actually controls before proposing implementation.

**What the gateway is doing today**: every quote goes through ~6 hard gates (allowlist, deviation guard, platform status, mapping, confidence floor, kill switches) any of which can `REJECT`. The first canary run had 0 accepts out of 48 because two of those gates (allowlist + deviation guard) bounce everything. There's no "quote wide instead of reject" path.

**What "user offers" means in our world**: every Hedix RFQ that lands on `/api/v1/rfq/quote` is a user offer. Today we're either accepting at a single price (one strategy, one set of knobs) or rejecting. We have no way to dial up willingness-to-quote vs. willingness-to-protect.

**My recommendation — risk profile, not a continuous knob.** A continuous "aggression float" sounds elegant but is operationally hostile (which knob moved? what's the current state?). Five named profiles, each a bundle of existing knobs, are easier to reason about, easier to A/B, easier to roll back. Mapping:

| Profile | Spread (markup) | Confidence floor | Deviation guard | Inventory cap | Low-conf path | Allowlist |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`conservative`** | wide (e.g. 50¢ markup) | 0.7 | tight (50¢) | small (50 contracts/superset) | reject | strict |
| **`balanced`** (default) | medium (25¢) | 0.5 | medium (100¢) | medium (200) | reject | curated |
| **`aggressive`** | tight (10¢) | 0.3 | loose (200¢) | large (1000) | reject | curated |
| **`always-quote`** | tight + sliding markup on confidence | 0.0 (any) | disabled | very large | quote-wide | none |
| **`accept-all`** | placeholder fallback (e.g. 500¢) | 0.0 | disabled | unbounded | quote-fallback | none |

Each profile = one env var (`RFQ_PROFILE=conservative`) that overrides the granular `RFQ_AS_*` knobs. Granular knobs still take precedence when explicitly set, so operators can override one knob without leaving the profile.

**Main tradeoff**: `always-quote` and `accept-all` need a new "quote-wide instead of reject" code path. Today every safety gate `Err`'s out of the handler. Moving them to "produce a degraded quote" is invasive — it changes the contract of the strategy/safety layer. Concretely:

- **conservative / balanced / aggressive** = ~1 PR (param bundle + a `Profile` enum that overrides `RfqConfig` + `AsParams` defaults). Low risk.
- **always-quote / accept-all** = ~3 PRs (refactor `RfqError` so the strategy layer can emit `QuoteWith { price_cents, confidence }` for cases that currently reject; add a `Markup::sliding` mode where markup grows as confidence drops; new strategy `AcceptAll` with a fixed-fallback). Medium risk — it weakens the safety floor by design, so we want the harness + backtest pipeline running first to spot money-losing profiles before they ship.

**Order I'd recommend:**
1. **Add `RFQ_PROFILE` enum + 3 conservative-end profiles** (conservative / balanced / aggressive) — operator can already pick aggression today without a code refactor. Pick `balanced` as the new default.
2. **Wire backtest module to consume real ring data** (the `Phase 4.1` TODO from the existing `src/backtest/mod.rs:8` comment). Now you can run "what would last week's traffic have made under each profile?" before flipping.
3. **Add `always-quote` profile** — needs the QuoteWith refactor. Run in parallel against `balanced` via a `partner_id`-based router for ~2 weeks, compare paper PnL.
4. **Add `accept-all` only after** the always-quote data shows we know how to widen markups under stress. This is the most dangerous setting.

Before I touch code: which path do you want to prioritize? And: do you have a concrete first counterparty in mind (e.g., Hedix is going live with real partners on date X), or is this prep for a longer horizon? The former changes scope — we'd need profile #1 ready in days; the latter we can sequence properly.

## Assistant · 2026-04-27T12:03:59

Going through every knob in the live config, what it does, what we observed today, and which way to nudge it for different goals.

## Tier 1 — Pricing core (Avellaneda-Stoikov)

The A-S model produces two outputs per quote: a **reservation price** (the inventory-skewed midpoint we want) and a **half-spread** (the markup we add to either side).

```
reservation_price = FV_mid - q · γ · σ² · T
half_spread       = γ · σ² · T  +  (2/γ) · ln(1 + γ/k̂)
```

| Knob | Current | Meaning | Today's evidence | Lever for |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RFQ_AS_GAMMA` (γ) | **0.1** | Risk aversion. Larger γ → wider spread *and* more inventory skew. | Half-spread observed = 24¢. With γ=0.1, k̂=0.01: `(2/γ)·ln(1+γ/k̂) = 20·ln(11) ≈ 48` → 24¢ half-spread. Math matches the wire. | Bump to 0.2 → ~30¢ half-spread, doubles inventory skew per contract. **More conservative.** |
| `RFQ_AS_SIGMA0` (σ₀) | **0.007** | Base volatility (per √time, in fraction-of-1000c). Drives the σ²·T term of the spread. | At σ₀=0.007, the σ² term contributes <1¢ to spread — k-hat term dominates. So σ₀ is currently a no-op for spread. | Only matters if you raise σ₀ to ≥0.05. At that point spread starts to scale with vol — useful pre-earnings or other event windows. |
| `RFQ_AS_K_HAT` (k̂) | **0.01** | Order-arrival decay. Larger k̂ = more arrivals near mid → tighter spread is profitable. Smaller k̂ = arrivals robust to distance → wider spread is fine. | The dominant term in our spread today. Halve it (0.005) → half-spread ~61¢. Double it (0.02) → half-spread ~12¢. | The "spread tightness" knob. Keep at 0.01 unless you observe fill rates crashing or surging. |
| `RFQ_AS_VPIN_KILL_THRESHOLD` | **0.5** | Adverse-selection kill switch. VPIN > 0.5 (50% buy/sell volume imbalance over a window) → reject. | Inactive — no fills triggered VPIN today. Polymarket trade flow on these tickers is too quiet for VPIN to read meaningfully. | Tighten to 0.3 if you start getting picked off (negative +60s drift on fills). |
| `RFQ_AS_DIVERGENCE_KILL_THRESHOLD_CENTS` | **100** | Cross-venue divergence kill — fires when our sources disagree by >N¢. | **Inactive** — we have only one source (Polymarket) per superset. Won't fire until a second source is wired. | Add a second source (Kalshi? proprietary feed?) for divergence to mean anything. |

## Tier 2 — Inventory & risk

| Knob | Current | Meaning | Today's evidence | Lever for |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RFQ_AS_MAX_INVENTORY` | **500** | Hard cap on signed inventory per superset. Exceed → strategy rejects with `inventory_cap:q_X_cap_Y`. | Was 50 → was the binding bottleneck. After 6 fills × ~17 contracts net, q hit 101 vs cap 50, rejected everything. **This was the single highest-impact change of the night.** | The "exposure budget" knob. R$10k total budget ÷ N markets = R$2.5k/market = ~500 contracts (at ~$5 mid). Scale linearly with market count. |
| `RFQ_AS_SOFT_CAP_START` | **100** | Inventory level (in absolute contracts) where price-skew kicks in. Linear ramp from `soft_cap_start` to `max_inventory`. | Was 10 (against cap=50, so 20%). Bumped to 100 to maintain the 20% proportion against cap=500. | Keep ≈ 20% of `MAX_INVENTORY`. If you go more conservative, shrink the ratio (10%) to skew earlier. |

## Tier 3 — Data-quality gates

These are the *no-quote* gates that fire before pricing even runs.

| Knob | Current | Meaning | Today's evidence | Lever for |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RFQ_FAIR_VALUE_MAX_AGE_SECS` | **300** | Reject when cached FV is older than this. | Was 180. AMZN/MSFT cache entries refresh every 90-200s, occasionally aging past 180s. Bumping to 300 cleared the `last_update_age_229s` rejections. **This was the second highest-impact change of the night.** | If Polymarket's WS feed gets faster (or you add a faster source), tighten back toward 60-120s. If feed gets quieter, loosen to 600s. |
| `RFQ_FAIR_VALUE_MIN_CONFIDENCE` | **0.08** | Reject when book-depth-derived confidence < N (range [0,1]). | Live cache today: AAPL 0.235, GOOGL 0.313, AMZN 0.127, MSFT 0.119. **All four would still pass at 0.1; only GOOGL+AAPL would pass at 0.2.** Kept at 0.08 to keep all 4 markets quotable. | Operationally useful as a thin-book filter. The right level is the *minimum* confidence among supersets you care about. |
| `RFQ_TICKER_ALLOWLIST` | **4 markets** (AAPL/MSFT/GOOGL/AMZN-2026APR17) | Hard whitelist; non-listed tickers reject with `TICKER_NOT_ALLOWED`. | 8 of 12 Hedix markets bounce here today (no Polymarket mapping). Cheap rejection — fast 422 with no strategy work. | Per-market kill switch. Removing one is operationally instant: `fly secrets set RFQ_TICKER_ALLOWLIST=...` (no code change). |
| `RFQ_MAX_PRICE_DEVIATION_CENTS` | **unset** | When set, rejects quotes more than N cents from `last_trade_price_cents`. Fails closed when the field is null. | Critical that this is unset — staging Hedix markets have null `last_trade_price_cents`, so any value here would reject everything. **Memory mistake earlier**: I had documented this as the cause of staging rejections, but it was actually inactive — A-S inventory cap was the real cause. | Re-enable only when staging starts populating `last_trade_price_cents` AND we trust those values (it's a circular sanity check — Hedix's last trade is partially set by *us*). |

## Tier 4 — Operational

| Knob | Current | Meaning | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `RFQ_STRATEGY` | `avellaneda_stoikov` | Switches the active pricing engine: `placeholder` (fixed price), `fair_value` (FV mid + flat markup), or `avellaneda_stoikov` (full inventory-aware). | A-S is the most expressive but most knob-rich. `fair_value` is a simpler fallback if A-S misbehaves. `placeholder` is for emergency operations only. |
| `HEDIX_BASE_URL` | `api.staging.hedix.com.br` | Hedix MM API root. | Tomorrow stays staging per your call. Production is `api.hedix.com.br`. |
| `HEDIX_API_KEY` | (MM key, app secret) | Auth for outbound to Hedix. | One MM account; same key works for prod when we cut over. |
| `HEDIX_ENV` | `staging` | Used in interaction-log metadata; informational. | |
| `HEDIX_WRITE_ENABLED` | **0** | Gates the `create_intents` write path inside `SafetyLayer::pre_write`. **Does NOT affect the RFQ accept path** — Hedix builds the counter-order from our response body regardless. | `=0` is correct for tomorrow. Only flip to `=1` if/when we add a proactive market-making path (separate from RFQ). |
| `RFQ_API_KEY` | (gateway secret) | Single key for both `/api/v1/rfq/quote` (Hedix → us) and `/admin/*` (us → us). | Same key the canary uses. |
| `RFQ_PORT` | 8080 | Internal listen port; matches `fly.toml [http_service].internal_port`. | |
| `RFQ_MAPPING_FILE` | `docs/mapping.yaml` | Path to the Hedix-superset → Polymarket-token mapping. Loaded at startup. | Editing requires redeploy (path is `include_str!`-ed via the binary's WORKDIR). 6 supersets mapped, 14 explicitly `no_upstream`. |
| `RFQ_POLYMARKET_WS_ENABLED` | 1 | Toggles the WebSocket feed (`wss://ws-subscriptions-clob.polymarket.com/ws/market`). | Without WS, FV cache only updates via REST polls (slower). Today's 90-200s cadence per superset *is* with WS on — investigate post-launch. |

## Tier 5 — Observability rings (defaults, not currently overridden)

All four ring sizes default to 1024 entries. None set as Fly secrets — change requires `fly secrets set` + restart.

| Knob | Default | What's in the ring | When to grow |
|---|---|---|---|
| `RFQ_INBOUND_REQUEST_CAPACITY` | 1024 | Every inbound RFQ + its response | If you exceed 1024 RFQs between admin pulls |
| `RFQ_FILL_OBSERVATION_CAPACITY` | 1024 | Each accepted quote + +60s drift back-fill | Same |
| `RFQ_REJECTION_LOG_CAPACITY` | 1024 | Each rejected RFQ with code + reason | Same |
| `RFQ_FV_HISTORY_CAPACITY_PER_SUPERSET` | 1024 | Time-series of FV updates per superset | Per-superset. Bump if you want minutes-of-history rather than just-now |

## What we **didn't** touch tonight (and why)

- **`RFQ_AS_GAMMA` and `RFQ_AS_K_HAT`** — these jointly produce the 24¢ half-spread, which is the right answer for today's traffic shape. Changing one affects the other's effective leverage; tune as a pair, not individually.
- **`RFQ_AS_SIGMA0`** — currently a no-op. Don't touch unless you also raise it past ~0.03 where the σ²·T term starts mattering.
- **`RFQ_FAIR_VALUE_MIN_CONFIDENCE`** — bumping to 0.2 would have killed MSFT and AMZN coverage. Keep low until/unless those Polymarket books deepen.

## Two-axis "aggression" intuition

Re-grouping into the conservative ↔ accept-all spectrum we discussed earlier:

| Knob | Conservative direction | Aggressive direction |
|---|---|---|
| `MAX_INVENTORY` | Lower (smaller exposure) | Higher (more capital at work) |
| `SOFT_CAP_START` | Lower (skew kicks in earlier) | Higher (later) |
| `GAMMA` | Higher (wider spread, more skew) | Lower (tighter spread) |
| `K_HAT` | Lower (wider spread justified) | Higher (tighter) |
| `SIGMA0` | Higher (wider spread) | Lower (no-op below 0.03) |
| `MIN_CONFIDENCE` | Higher (refuse thin books) | Lower (quote anything) |
| `MAX_AGE_SECS` | Lower (refuse stale FV) | Higher (tolerate stale) |
| `VPIN_KILL_THRESHOLD` | Lower (kill on mild toxicity) | Higher (only kill on flagrant) |
| `TICKER_ALLOWLIST` | Smaller list | Empty (no list = all allowed) |
| `MAX_PRICE_DEVIATION_CENTS` | Set tight (e.g., 50) | Unset (no guard) |

Today we're sitting at: **medium-conservative on inventory, medium-aggressive on spread tightness, very-aggressive on confidence floor, medium on staleness.** Tomorrow's traffic will tell us which axis to nudge first.

## Assistant · 2026-04-27T12:09:13

Reframe before scenarios — "first quote" on Hedix means **proactive intents** (`POST /api/mm/intents`), not RFQ responses. The gateway's RFQ flow is *reactive* — it waits for users to ask. To be the first sitting price on the book, you place SELL YES + SELL NO intents directly. Two-track plan:

| Track | Markets | Mechanism |
|---|---|---|
| **RFQ-driven** (gateway picks the price live) | The 4 mapped: AAPL, MSFT, GOOGL, AMZN | Already running tonight's config — A-S quotes ±24¢ around Polymarket FV |
| **Intents-driven** (you set the price tonight) | The 8 unmapped: TESLA, NVIDIA, USDBRLL, OUROO, PETR4, VALE3, NU, ETHEREUM | Direct `POST /api/mm/intents` from a script with hand-curated prior mids |

The gateway can't quote the 8 unmapped — no Polymarket mapping → no FV → A-S returns reject. So intents are the only way to be first there. **They don't conflict** with the RFQ path because the gateway never sees them.

## Per-market prior mids (you adjust before placing)

These are my best-effort priors. **You almost certainly know the Brazilian / commodity markets better than I do — overwrite freely.** Format: `<superset> | mid_yes_centavos = "I think YES probability is N%"`.

| Ticker | Question | My prior mid (¢) | My implied P(YES) | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TESLA-2026APR17-450 | TSLA > $450 by May 20? | **300** | 30% | low — depends on recent TSLA trajectory |
| NVIDIA-2026APR17-250 | NVDA > $250 by May 20? | **450** | 45% | low — post-split NVDA was around $200 in 2025 |
| USDBRLL-2026APR17-5.5 | USD/BRL > 5.5 by May 20? | **500** | 50% | medium — BRL bounces around 5.0-6.0 |
| OUROO-2026APR17-5.0 | Gold > 5.0 ??? by May 20 | **400** | 40% | **need clarification** — what's the unit? |
| PETR4-2026APR17-50 | PETR4 > R$50 by May 20? | **250** | 25% | low — PETR4 has been in 30s |
| VALE3-2026APR17-95 | VALE3 > R$95 by May 20? | **175** | 17% | low — VALE3 has been in 60s-70s |
| NU-2026APR17-18 | NU > $18 by May 20? | **200** | 20% | low — NU has been in 10-15 range |
| ETHEREUM-2026APR17-2500 | ETH > $2500 by May 20? | **600** | 60% | low — ETH price changes fast |

Anywhere you have stronger conviction, narrow the spread. Anywhere you don't, widen it.

## Four scenarios (intents-only — for the 8 unmapped)

For each scenario I list: **half-spread**, **quantity per side**, **total per-market max collateral**, **realistic fill expectation**. All using a placeholder mid M (you fill in from the table above).

### Scenario 1 — Sniper (max coverage, near-zero downside)

**One side at extreme prices**, tiny size.

```
SELL YES @ 990¢, qty 1 (collateral: 1000-990 = 10¢ × 1 = R$0.10)
SELL NO  @ 990¢, qty 1 (collateral: 10¢ × 1 = R$0.10)
```

- **Per-market exposure**: R$0.20
- **Total across 8 markets**: R$1.60
- **Fills**: only when a buyer is genuinely desperate — but you ARE on every book first. PnL: ~zero.
- **Use case**: priority is "be first" not "make money".

### Scenario 2 — Conservative (wide spreads around best-guess mid)

**±200¢ around your prior**, small size.

```
SELL YES @ M+200, qty 5  (collateral: (1000-M-200) × 5)
SELL NO  @ (1000-M)+200, qty 5  (collateral: (M-200) × 5)
```

For TESLA (M=300): SELL YES @ 500¢ qty 5 (collateral R$25), SELL NO @ 900¢ qty 5 (collateral R$5). **Per-market max R$30**, total ~R$240 across 8.

- **Fills**: ~5-15% of touchers. Per-fill edge ~150¢ if your mid was right.
- **Use case**: wide enough that bad priors don't kill you; you'll only fill on enthusiastic counterparties.

### Scenario 3 — Balanced (±100¢, mid size)

```
SELL YES @ M+100, qty 25  (collateral: (1000-M-100) × 25)
SELL NO  @ (1000-M)+100, qty 25  (collateral: (M-100) × 25)
```

For TESLA (M=300): SELL YES @ 400 qty 25 (collateral R$150), SELL NO @ 800 qty 25 (collateral R$50). **Per-market R$200**, total ~R$1.6k across 8.

- **Fills**: ~30-50% if there's any traffic. Per-fill edge ~70¢ if mid is right; -100c+ if mid was off.
- **Use case**: confident-enough priors, want some PnL while still capping risk.

### Scenario 4 — Aggressive (±50¢, larger size)

```
SELL YES @ M+50, qty 100  (collateral: (1000-M-50) × 100)
SELL NO  @ (1000-M)+50, qty 100  (collateral: (M-50) × 100)
```

For TESLA (M=300): SELL YES @ 350 qty 100 (collateral R$650), SELL NO @ 750 qty 100 (collateral R$250). **Per-market R$900**, total ~R$7.2k across 8.

- **Fills**: most of them. Per-fill edge ~25¢ if mid is right; **-150¢+ if your mid was off** by 100¢.
- **Use case**: confident priors, want fill volume. Risky on markets you don't really know.

## My recommendation: hybrid

| Confidence level | Scenario | Markets |
|---|---|---|
| **High** prior conviction | 3 (Balanced) | USDBRLL, ETHEREUM (you know FX/crypto) |
| **Medium** | 2 (Conservative) | NU, PETR4, VALE3 (Brazilian equity, you have edge) |
| **Low** | 1 (Sniper) | TESLA, NVIDIA, OUROO (US equity / unclear unit) |

Total commitment: ~R$1k across 8 unmapped markets, capped exposure on the things you're least sure about.

Plus the RFQ path for the 4 mapped (AAPL, MSFT, GOOGL, AMZN) handles itself with the live A-S config — current canary shows +27¢ mean edge there.

## How to actually place the intents

Same key, same Hedix endpoint that the harness probes. Template you (or a script) can run:

```bash
# Set once
KEY=<your HEDIX_API_KEY>

# Per intent (repeat for each side of each market)
curl -sS -X POST https://api.staging.hedix.com.br/api/mm/intents \
  -H "X-API-Key: $KEY" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{
    "intents": [{
      "market_ticker": "TESLA-2026APR17-450",
      "side": "SELL",
      "outcome": "YES",
      "quantity": 5,
      "price_cents": 500,
      "valid_for_seconds": 36000,
      "client_intent_id": "first-quote-tesla-yes-20260428"
    }]
  }'
```

Notes:
- `valid_for_seconds: 36000` = 10 hours — covers the launch window. The Hedix server enforces a per-MM minimum floor; if 36000 is too high it'll be clamped.
- `client_intent_id` must be unique per intent — embed market+side+date so retries are idempotent.
- One intent per request keeps the response easy to read; the API supports up to 2 in a batch (same market).

## Three checks before you fire

1. **The R$1.00 minimum collateral floor** — every intent must have `qty × max(price, 1000-price) ≥ 100`. The Sniper scenario at qty=1 violates this (collateral 10¢). Bump to qty=10 or price-step to 900 to satisfy.
2. **Confirm `OUROO`'s unit** — gold/ouro at "5.0" is ambiguous (per gram? per oz? in BRL? USD?). Don't quote until you're sure what it means.
3. **The 4 mapped markets** — you don't need to place intents on AAPL/MSFT/GOOGL/AMZN. The gateway's RFQ flow is already responding with +24¢ to +34¢ edge. Layering intents on top would just compete with our own RFQ responses.

When you've picked scenarios per market and have prices confirmed, want me to generate the full curl block (16 intents = 8 markets × 2 sides) for you to copy-paste? Faster than typing them.

Reframe so we agree: "user orders" = orders **you place from a Hedix user account** that get routed to our MM gateway as RFQs. This validates the full Hedix → gateway path that the canary skips. Goal is to catch any prod-routing surprises before real users fire at 09:00.

## Test matrix — 10 orders

Designed so each row tells you something different. **Run in order**; each phase depends on the previous working.

### Phase 1: Smoke — basic quote works on every mapped market (4 orders)

| # | Ticker | Side | Qty | Expected price | Why this test |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `AAPL-2026APR17-290` | YES | 10 | ~109¢ (FV 85 + 24¢ markup) | Cheapest market — small absolute price means tight tolerance |
| 2 | `MSFT-2026APR17-420` | YES | 10 | ~459¢ (FV 435 + 24¢) | At-the-money market |
| 3 | `GOOGL-2026APR17-320` | YES | 10 | ~944¢ (FV 920 + 24¢) | Deep ITM market — exercises 1-999 ceiling on prices |
| 4 | `AMZN-2026APR17-258` | YES | 10 | ~384¢ (FV 360 + 24¢) | The one with strike-skew (Polymarket $260 vs Hedix $258) |

After these 4, hit `/admin/fills` and verify exactly 4 entries with `our_price_cents` within ±5¢ of expected.

### Phase 2: NO-side coverage (4 orders)

| # | Ticker | Side | Qty | Expected price | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5 | `AAPL` | NO | 10 | ~942¢ (FV_no 915 + 27¢) | NO side has different math; catches the asymmetric markup |
| 6 | `MSFT` | NO | 10 | ~599¢ | |
| 7 | `GOOGL` | NO | 10 | ~107¢ (FV_no 80 + 27¢) | NO of high-probability YES — small absolute |
| 8 | `AMZN` | NO | 10 | ~674¢ | |

After these, `/admin/fills` should show **8 entries**, mean `+27¢` edge captured.

### Phase 3: Edge cases (3 orders)

| # | Ticker | Side | Qty | Expected outcome | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 9 | `TESLA-2026APR17-450` | YES | 10 | **REJECT** `TICKER_NOT_ALLOWED` | Confirm allowlist gate fires fast; confirms 8 unmapped markets behave as documented |
| 10 | `AAPL` | YES | 600 | **REJECT** `inventory_cap:q_X_cap_500` | Confirms the 500 cap works after we've already accepted ~50 contracts of YES this morning |
| 11 *(optional)* | `AAPL` | YES | 100 | Higher than #1's price (soft-cap skew) | Inventory at +50 already from #1 + #5 + #9-attempts; further YES should price up due to skew |

## Why this matrix

- **Both sides on each mapped market** → we know the asymmetric NO-side markup formula is right (we just fixed it tonight in the harness; this confirms the gateway itself is correct).
- **Each market has different price magnitude** → tests we don't have a `1-999` clamp bug or a price-side confusion. GOOGL YES at 944 + AAPL NO at 942 are the closest to the 999 ceiling.
- **The qty=10 baseline** → above the R$1.00 collateral floor, below the soft-cap-start of 100, so no skew confounds the price prediction.
- **Phase 3 #9** → unmapped reject is the dominant rejection users will see tomorrow on the 8 markets we don't quote. Worth confirming the partner-facing message is the one Hedix expects.
- **Phase 3 #10** → without this, you don't know the inventory cap works in prod (it works in canary — but the canary uses `partner_id: harness` and might be on a different code path I missed).
- **Phase 3 #11 (optional)** → the only way to *see* A-S inventory skewing live. Skip if time is short.

## What to verify after each phase

```bash
# After Phase 1: should show 4 entries, all 200, all YES
fly ssh console -C 'sh -c "curl -sS -H \"X-API-Key: \$RFQ_API_KEY\" http://localhost:8080/admin/inbound?limit=20"'

# After Phase 2: 8 fills total; mean edge ~+27¢
fly ssh console -C 'sh -c "curl -sS -H \"X-API-Key: \$RFQ_API_KEY\" http://localhost:8080/admin/fills?limit=20"'

# After Phase 3: 1 ticker_not_allowed + 1 inventory_cap reject
fly ssh console -C 'sh -c "curl -sS -H \"X-API-Key: \$RFQ_API_KEY\" http://localhost:8080/admin/rejections?limit=20"'
```

The `partner_id` field on each entry tells you whether the order came through your Hedix user account (will be Hedix's tag, not "harness").

## Red flags to look for

| Symptom | What it means | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| Phase 1 quote off by >50¢ from expected | Polymarket FV moved since 11:59Z baseline | Pull `/admin/intel/cache`, verify, recompute expected |
| Any Phase 1 returns 422 instead of 200 | Either FV aged out (>300s) or another reject path I didn't anticipate | Check `/admin/rejections` reason field |
| Phase 3 #9 returns anything other than `TICKER_NOT_ALLOWED` | Allowlist isn't matching exactly — possibly Hedix sends a slightly different ticker string | Look at the inbound entry; copy the exact ticker into `RFQ_TICKER_ALLOWLIST` |
| Phase 3 #10 returns 200 instead of rejecting | Inventory cap not enforced — strategy bug or env var not picked up | Look at `/admin/intel/inventory` — should show ~50 from prior fills, but the qty=600 should still hit cap |
| `partner_id` is missing/wrong | Hedix isn't sending the `X-Partner-Id` header we expect | Coordinate with Hedix; either they fix it or we accept any value (we already default to "hedix") |

## What to do if Phase 1 passes but Phase 2 fails

This would mean YES side works but NO side doesn't. Most likely cause: the strategy's NO-leg pricing has a bug we haven't seen because the canary's only failure was harness-side (now fixed). Roll back to RFQ_STRATEGY=fair_value (simpler markup) as a holding pattern:

```bash
fly secrets set --app artelonga-rfq-gateway RFQ_STRATEGY=fair_value
```

Quotes will be FV ± a fixed markup. Less elegant than A-S but provably correct.

## Time budget

10 orders, ~30s each (place → see response in Hedix UI → check admin) = ~5 min total. If Hedix UI is slow, 15 min. Comfortable margin before 09:00.

**Want me to also draft a fallback plan for the 8 unmapped markets** (TESLA/NVIDIA/USDBRLL/OUROO/PETR4/VALE3/NU/ETHEREUM) if a real user RFQs them tomorrow? Today they all bounce with `TICKER_NOT_ALLOWED` — Hedix's UI will show "no MM responded" or similar. If you want graceful coverage on those, we'd need the Polymarket mappings (research) or an `RFQ_STRATEGY=placeholder` fallback (code change).

## Test budget

12 orders, each ~30s round-trip on Hedix's UI. ~6 min total. After all 12:

| Endpoint | Expected counts |
|---|---|
| `/admin/inbound` | 12 entries (8 accepts, 2 rejects, 1 large-fill, 1 skew-fill) |
| `/admin/fills` | 10 fills (9 if skew test fires after I) — mean edge ~+27¢ |
| `/admin/rejections` | 2 entries: `inventory_cap:*`, `ticker_not_allowed` |
| `/admin/intel/inventory` | non-zero q on AAPL (~−110), small q on MSFT/GOOGL/AMZN |

## When to stop

- **Test A fails** → stop, diagnose. Don't attempt B-L until A is green.
- **Test E fails (price wildly off)** → stop, NO-side bug; flip strategy to `fair_value` as fallback (`fly secrets set RFQ_STRATEGY=fair_value`).
- **Test J accepts (no cap rejection)** → stop, rollback (`fly secrets set RFQ_AS_MAX_INVENTORY=50`); inventory budget isn't enforced.
- **Test K accepts** → stop, unmapped market unsafely quoted somehow; tighten allowlist or strategy.
- **All others pass** → ship-ready, monitor real traffic from 09:00.

Each test produces its own audit trail in the rings — pull `/admin/inbound?limit=20` after each batch (after D, after H, after L) to verify inputs match what was sent.

## Assistant · 2026-04-27T12:48:42

Confirmed: `api/rfq-api.yaml:185-218` — **the inbound RFQ has only `ticker`, `side`, `quantity`, `auto_accept`. No price field.** Hedix doesn't tell us the user's limit. That's the whole answer to "why didn't we fill at 200" — we structurally cannot.

## What actually happens

```
┌─────────┐  user limit @ 200      ┌────────┐                 ┌──────────┐
│  USER   │ ────────────────────► │ HEDIX  │ RFQ (no price)→ │ GATEWAY  │
└─────────┘                        │        │ ◄── 111 ──────  │  (us)    │
     ▲                             │        │                 └──────────┘
     │  fill @ 111 (not 200)       │        │
     └──────────────────────────── │        │
                                   └────────┘
```

Hedix is the matching engine. They see both sides. They use **price improvement** semantics: cross at the MM's quote (better for the user), not the user's limit. The user's 200 only controls **whether** the trade happens, never the price. This is intentional protocol design — it incentivizes users to set generous limits without fear of being charged the limit. If we *could* fill at the user's limit, users would learn to bid tight (e.g., 112 instead of 200), and our edge would converge to zero anyway.

## So how do we extract more?

Three levers, each with a trade-off:

**1. Widen our spread.** Bump `RFQ_AS_GAMMA` 0.1 → 0.2 → half-spread doubles to ~48¢. We'd quote 87 + 48 = 135¢ instead of 111¢. Still fills against a 200 limit, but **rejects against a 130 limit** (users with tight limits see no MM available). Per-fill edge nearly doubles; fill rate drops by however many users were sitting in the 110-135 band.

**2. Decrease k̂ (arrival rate).** `RFQ_AS_K_HAT` 0.01 → 0.005 → half-spread ~61¢, quote ~148¢. More aggressive than (1). Same trade-off, more pronounced.

**3. Stop using A-S, use placeholder.** `RFQ_STRATEGY=placeholder` with a high fixed price (say 500¢ on every market). Captures the maximum surplus on every fill that crosses, but **mispriced absolutely** — we'd happily sell deep-OTM contracts at 500¢ that should be ~50¢. Catastrophic if a sophisticated user shows up.

**The right answer for tomorrow morning is none of the above.** We've shown +24¢ edge per fill with predictable behavior on real Hedix traffic. Until we observe the *shape* of user limits (do they bid 112¢ or 200¢?), widening spreads is guessing. Tomorrow's traffic is the data we need. Tuesday's tuning decision will be informed.

What we CAN do post-launch if the data warrants:

- If users mostly set wide limits (200+ when our quote is 110) → widen γ to 0.15-0.2 and capture more
- If users mostly set tight limits (112 when our quote is 110) → keep γ at 0.1, our spread is already at the natural ceiling
- If competition arrives (other MMs quote 105 or 100) → tighten γ to 0.05 to win flow

This is a measurement problem, not a design problem. Tomorrow's `/admin/inbound` data will tell us where users actually sit.

## Verification scripts

Drop these in your shell as functions. Each is one round trip. Run after every test order.

**`vlast`** — show the latest inbound + fill in one go (best for confirming "was that us?"):

```bash
vlast() {
  fly ssh console --app artelonga-rfq-gateway -C "sh -c '
    echo \"=== INBOUND ===\";
    curl -sS -H \"X-API-Key: \$RFQ_API_KEY\" http://localhost:8080/admin/inbound?limit=1;
    echo;
    echo \"=== FILL ===\";
    curl -sS -H \"X-API-Key: \$RFQ_API_KEY\" http://localhost:8080/admin/fills?limit=1;
  '" | tail -20
}
```

**`vstate`** — current state across all rings (run periodically; great mid-test):

```bash
vstate() {
  fly ssh console --app artelonga-rfq-gateway -C "sh -c '
    echo \"=== inbound count ===\";
    curl -sS -H \"X-API-Key: \$RFQ_API_KEY\" http://localhost:8080/admin/inbound?limit=1 | grep -oE \"\\\"count\\\":[0-9]+\";
    echo \"=== fills count ===\";
    curl -sS -H \"X-API-Key: \$RFQ_API_KEY\" http://localhost:8080/admin/fills?limit=1 | grep -oE \"\\\"count\\\":[0-9]+\";
    echo \"=== rejections count ===\";
    curl -sS -H \"X-API-Key: \$RFQ_API_KEY\" http://localhost:8080/admin/rejections?limit=1 | grep -oE \"\\\"count\\\":[0-9]+\";
    echo \"=== inventory ===\";
    curl -sS -H \"X-API-Key: \$RFQ_API_KEY\" http://localhost:8080/admin/intel/inventory;
  '" | tail -30
}
```

**`vreject`** — show last reject with reason (use after deliberate-reject tests J, K):

```bash
vreject() {
  fly ssh console --app artelonga-rfq-gateway -C 'sh -c "curl -sS -H \"X-API-Key: \$RFQ_API_KEY\" http://localhost:8080/admin/rejections?limit=1"' | tail -1 | tr "}" "\n" | grep -E "code|reason|message|ticker|quantity"
}
```

**`vfv`** — current Polymarket fair-value cache (sanity-check before each test):

```bash
vfv() {
  fly ssh console --app artelonga-rfq-gateway -C 'sh -c "curl -sS -H \"X-API-Key: \$RFQ_API_KEY\" http://localhost:8080/admin/intel/cache"' | tail -1 | tr "}" "\n" | grep -E "superset|price_cents|confidence"
}
```

If you have these in your shell, the test loop becomes:

```
1. vfv                    # see current FV per market
2. [place order in Hedix]
3. vlast                  # confirm it's us, see our quote
4. vstate                 # totals updated
```

## Assistant · 2026-04-27T13:00:18

Got it — generous launch profile, prioritize fill rate over per-fill edge. Math first, then apply.

## What "generous" means in our knobs

**Spread tightening — the biggest lever.** A-S half-spread formula at our k̂=0.01 is dominated by `(2/γ)·ln(1+γ/k̂)`. *Increasing* γ tightens spread in this regime:

| γ | Half-spread |
|---|---|
| 0.1 (current) | ~24¢ |
| **0.2** | **~15¢** |
| 0.3 | ~11¢ |
| 0.5 | ~8¢ |

γ=0.2 is the sweet spot: ~38% tighter quote, modest inventory skew. γ=0.3+ starts skewing aggressively as inventory builds.

## Proposed bundle

```
RFQ_AS_GAMMA              0.1  → 0.2    # tighter spreads (~15¢ half-spread)
RFQ_AS_MAX_INVENTORY      500  → 1000   # 2x capacity → fewer cap rejections
RFQ_AS_SOFT_CAP_START     100  → 200    # 20% of new cap
RFQ_AS_VPIN_KILL_THRESHOLD 0.5 → 0.7    # less aggressive adverse-selection kill
RFQ_FAIR_VALUE_MIN_CONFIDENCE 0.08 → 0.05  # quote thinner Polymarket books
```

What stays:
- Strategy: avellaneda_stoikov (don't lose pricing intelligence)
- σ₀ 0.007 (no-op currently, no point touching)
- Staleness floor 300s (already loose)
- Allowlist still 4 mapped markets (8 unmapped need code+mapping work — post-launch)

**Expected effects after this:**
- Quotes ~9¢ tighter (AAPL YES ~102 vs 111, MSFT ~535 vs 544, etc.)
- Per-fill edge: +15¢ instead of +24¢ (-37%)
- Fill rate against tight user limits: significantly higher
- Inventory cap kicks in at 1000 contracts/superset (R$5k/market × 4 markets = R$20k total ceiling)
- Rolling restart will clear stale inventory state from this morning's tests

## Assistant · 2026-04-27T13:07:39

**Major finding — the model was wrong.**

`/api/mm/positions` returns `[]` — Hedix says we hold zero contracts. The "fills" we've been recording in our ring are **not actual fills**. They're SELL LIMIT INTENTS Hedix creates on its book at our quoted price. They sit there 5 min, fill if a buyer matches them, otherwise CANCEL/EXPIRE.

```
MSFT intent from test B (12:51:37):
  status: CANCELLED
  cancellation_reason: EXPIRED
  filled_quantity: 0
  client_intent_id: 00bcc3cf...   ← matches our quote_id
```

Same for the AMZN intents from this morning's canary — all 4 visible are CANCELLED/EXPIRED.

## What this means

| Our gateway thinks | Reality on Hedix |
|---|---|
| `/admin/fills` count = 11 | maybe 0-1 of those actually filled |
| `/admin/intel/inventory` shows q=+1 AAPL, +1 MSFT | actual q=0 across all supersets |
| Inventory cap was binding → bumped 50→500→1000 | the cap was binding on **phantom data** all along |
| "Mean edge captured: +27¢" | edge on quotes, not fills — meaningless |

The `auto_accept=true` flag from Hedix doesn't actually mean "trade just happened" — it means "place a SELL intent at this price for me". The user can decline → intent expires.

Let me check whether test A (AAPL) actually filled, since user limit 200 ≥ our 111 should have crossed:

## Assistant · 2026-04-27T13:10:53

Hedix shows 9 total intents — none from AAPL test A (12:42 today). But test B's MSFT intent IS there (CANCELLED EXPIRED). So either:
- AAPL test A never made it to Hedix's books, OR
- It filled but is on a different ledger (FILLED intents may not appear in this `?status=` listing)

## Assistant · 2026-04-27T13:13:18

The picture is now clear. **Nothing has ever actually filled.**

```
Hedix MM balance:     R$8832.20
Hedix MM available:   R$8832.20   ← all liquid
Hedix MM reserved:    R$0.00       ← zero collateral committed
Hedix MM positions:   []           ← empty
Test A quote_id:      404 NOT FOUND on Hedix
Test B intent:        CANCELLED, EXPIRED, filled_quantity=0
Filled-status query:  total: 0 across all markets, all time
```

## What our gateway is actually tracking

The `/admin/fills` ring records a "fill" the moment we respond to an RFQ with `auto_accept=true`. **That's not a fill — it's a quote.** Hedix translates our auto_accept response into a SELL LIMIT INTENT on its book. That intent then either:

- **Matches** an existing user BUY order and fills (would show in `?status=FILLED`)
- **Sits unmatched** until expiration (5-min TTL, then CANCELLED/EXPIRED)
- **Never appears** on Hedix's books (test A — quote_id 404; mechanism unclear)

In all our recorded "fills" so far: zero of them landed in option 1.

## The canary is even more decoupled

The canary calls `http://localhost:8080/api/v1/rfq/quote` directly — **bypasses Hedix entirely**. Those "fills" are gateway-talking-to-itself with no real-world consequence. That's why none of the harness's 100+ "fills" appear in Hedix's records.

## What this means for inventory cap

The inventory cap (1000 contracts/superset) is **gating real fills based on phantom data**. If our gateway thinks q=−500 from prior phantom fills, a real fill that would only push q to −600 (well within Hedix's actual capability) gets rejected. **The bottleneck isn't risk management; it's bookkeeping.**

Real risk management is on Hedix's side: they hold our R$8832 balance, they'd reject a SELL intent if it'd over-commit. We don't need our cap as a safety net.

## Urgent fix for launch (1 line)

```bash
fly secrets set --app artelonga-rfq-gateway RFQ_AS_MAX_INVENTORY=999999
```

Effectively disables the cap as a constraint. Hedix's collateral system becomes the only safety floor — which is correct, because they actually know our balance.

Soft cap (`SOFT_CAP_START=200`) still kicks in for *price skewing*, which is desired — but no longer rejects.

## What to monitor instead of `/admin/fills`

For ground truth tomorrow:

```bash
# Real positions
fly ssh console -C 'sh -c "curl -sS -H \"X-API-Key: \$HEDIX_API_KEY\" https://api.staging.hedix.com.br/api/mm/positions"'

# Real balance + reserved collateral  
fly ssh console -C 'sh -c "curl -sS -H \"X-API-Key: \$HEDIX_API_KEY\" https://api.staging.hedix.com.br/api/mm/balance"'

# Real intents (ours specifically) — filter for FILLED to see actual fills
fly ssh console -C 'sh -c "curl -sS -H \"X-API-Key: \$HEDIX_API_KEY\" \"https://api.staging.hedix.com.br/api/mm/intents?status=FILLED&limit=50\""'
```

The reconciler in our codebase already polls Hedix positions; `/admin/reconciliation` exposes its last snapshot.

## Post-launch fix (Tuesday)

The right code fix: don't increment `/admin/fills` on `auto_accept=true`. Instead:

1. Record the QUOTE (which is what we do today, but rename the ring to `/admin/quotes`)
2. Add a reconciliation task that polls Hedix's filled intents and **populates a real `/admin/fills` from Hedix's truth**
3. Inventory cap: gate on Hedix's actual position, not local count

This is ~50 LOC plus tests. Not safe to ship at 4am before launch.

## Should I apply the cap-bump now?

This is the only blocker for real fills tomorrow morning. The new γ=0.2 config + cap=999999 means: tighter quotes, no phantom-data rejections, real fills (or no fills) determined entirely by Hedix.

Confirm and I'll push it.

```bash

fly ssh console --app artelonga-rfq-gateway -C 'sh -c "curl -sS -H \"X-API-Key: \$RFQ_API_KEY\" http://localhost:8080/admin/inbound?limit=1"'

# Did Hedix actually fill? (real position appears)

# Real fills (Hedix-side truth)

```

If `positions` stays empty after a test that should fill → the trade didn't actually go through (intent didn't match; user's UI might show "cancelled").

| If | Then |
|---|---|
| A's quote >30¢ off prediction | Polymarket FV moved; pull `vfv` and adjust limits |
| Any of E/F/G/H rejects with `low_confidence` | Confidence dropped below 0.05; that superset is unquotable until book deepens |
| K fills (no allowlist reject) | Emergency rollback — `RFQ_TICKER_ALLOWLIST=NONE-DUMMY` |
| Hedix `positions` stays empty after multiple A-D fills | Crossing matching isn't working — investigate with Hedix |

Run order: **A → B → C → D → E → F → G → H → I → J → K → L → M**.

## Assistant · 2026-04-27T13:52:39

Applied. Now COPOM pricing — this needs careful framing because **Polymarket has zero coverage for SELIC**, so we need an alternative source.

# COPOM market pricing — the problem

Hedix's SELIC markets look like `SELIC-2026MAY28-13.25` — a **discrete-strike** market on the EXACT SELIC rate set at a specific COPOM meeting. Strikes are typically every 0.25% or 0.50% (e.g., 13.00, 13.25, 13.50). The contract pays R$10 if SELIC matches the strike exactly, otherwise zero.

This is fundamentally different from "AAPL above $290" which is a *continuous threshold* market priced cleanly from any continuous probability distribution. SELIC is **discrete and degenerate** — only one strike pays out, the rest are zeros.

The pricing problem has three nested layers:

1. **Where will SELIC be set?** A central forecast (e.g., 13.50%)
2. **How tight is the distribution?** Std. dev. of forecasts (e.g., σ = 12.5 bps)
3. **What's P(SELIC = exact strike)?** Discretize the continuous distribution into bin probabilities

Each strike's fair value is `1000 × P(SELIC = strike)`. Sum across strikes ≈ 1000 (a complete market).

# Five sources for a SELIC fair value

| Source | What it gives | Latency | Code complexity |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A. Boletim Focus (BCB)** | Median analyst forecast for next COPOM meeting | Updated weekly, Monday | API: `olinda.bcb.gov.br/.../Expectativas` — JSON. ~1h to wire |
| **B. B3 DI futures curve** | Forward-implied SELIC; the market's actual position | Real-time during B3 hours | API: B3 market data (paid) or B3 public end-of-day. Translation from DI rate → SELIC requires curve work. ~4h |
| **C. SELIC futures (DI1)** | Specific contract for COPOM date; cleanest implied rate | Real-time | Same as B but more focused. ~3h |
| **D. Hand-priced (operator)** | Operator types FVs into a YAML file | Manual — staleness depends on you | Trivial. 30 min code + ongoing operator work |
| **E. Hybrid (Focus + DI vol)** | Center from Focus, dispersion from DI implied vol | Daily refresh | Combines A+B. ~6h |

# What I'd actually recommend

**Tonight + tomorrow morning**: Don't quote COPOM. They're not in the allowlist. Hedix users hitting SELIC tickers will see `TICKER_NOT_ALLOWED` — same UX as TESLA/NVIDIA/etc. **No new code.**

**This week (Monday/Tuesday)**: Ship **option D first** — hand-priced. It's the only one we can run in 30 min:

1. Add `docs/manual_fv.yaml` (or extend `docs/mapping.yaml`):
   ```yaml
   - superset: SELIC-2026MAY28
     source: manual
     strikes:
       "12.75": { fair_value_cents: 50,  confidence: 0.5 }
       "13.00": { fair_value_cents: 200, confidence: 0.5 }
       "13.25": { fair_value_cents: 450, confidence: 0.6 }   # modal strike
       "13.50": { fair_value_cents: 250, confidence: 0.5 }
       "13.75": { fair_value_cents: 50,  confidence: 0.5 }
   ```

2. Strategy logic: when a superset has `source: manual`, look up the strike in the entry's `strikes` map and use that as fair value. Skip Polymarket lookup entirely.

3. Operator updates the YAML weekly after Boletim Focus drops, redeploys (rolling restart, ~30s).

This gets us **safe coverage on COPOM with operator judgment as the FV signal**. Spread on top is the same A-S markup as for AAPL/MSFT etc. — currently 11¢ at γ=0.3.

**This week+1 (Wednesday onward)**: Layer **option A** on top — pull Boletim Focus automatically, populate the same data structure. Operator overrides manual entries when needed.

**Later (next sprint)**: Add **option E** for the most accurate FV — Focus median + DI implied vol → kernel density → discrete strikes.

# The "kernel density" piece in plain words

Once we have a central forecast (X%) and dispersion (σ bps), turn it into per-strike probabilities by integrating a normal density across each strike's bucket:

```
For strike S:
  bucket_lo = S - 12.5 bps    (half a 25-bp step)
  bucket_hi = S + 12.5 bps
  P(SELIC = S) = Φ((bucket_hi - μ)/σ) − Φ((bucket_lo - μ)/σ)
  fair_value_cents = round(1000 × P)
```

Where μ = central forecast, σ = std. dev., Φ = normal CDF. About 30 LOC of pure math; no new dependencies.

# What I need from you to ship D

1. **Confirm Hedix's SELIC strike convention** — is it every 25 bps or 50 bps? `SELIC-2026MAY28-13.25` suggests 25 bp grid.
2. **A starting estimate for next COPOM** — your best read on where it'll land + how confident you are. Even a single number ("modal: 13.25, ±50 bps") is enough to populate the YAML.
3. **Whether to ship now or wait** — given we're already deep in tonight's launch prep, I'd vote for shipping D **after** tomorrow's first hour of trading data, when we've validated the equity-market path. Adding code paths now risks breaking what's working.

The code change is bounded (mostly a new strategy variant + YAML loader, ~150 LOC + tests). Want me to start it tonight as a separate PR that can sit unmerged until you flip the switch, or wait until after launch?

## Assistant · 2026-04-27T14:02:58

# COPOM pricing pipeline — design parallel to Polymarket

The existing Polymarket pipeline is the right shape; we extend it. The interfaces we already have (`FairValueCache`, `MarketDataSource`, `FairValueModel`, the A-S strategy) are source-agnostic. **What changes for SELIC is what feeds the cache, not what reads from it.**

## What's structurally different from equity-binary

Equity binary (`AAPL > $290 by date`) is a **threshold** — one number `P(YES)` is enough; spread bracketing the mid does the rest. SELIC strikes (`SELIC = 13.25 exactly`) are **discrete and zero-sum** — you must price *every strike in the meeting* simultaneously, and they sum to 1.0. A wrong distribution doesn't just cost you on one strike; you'll be too generous on the modal strike and too cheap on the tails (or vice versa) and bleed money systematically.

This is why we can't shortcut with "best guess" — we need an actual probability distribution.

## Five-stage pipeline (mirrors Polymarket)

### 1. Sources

| Source | What | Cadence | Cost | Reliability |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **BCB Boletim Focus** | Median + mean + std + min/max of analyst forecasts for next COPOM | Weekly (Mon ~9am BRT) | free | high — survey of ~140 institutions |
| **B3 DI futures** | Forward-implied SELIC from DI1 contract maturing on COPOM date | Real-time during B3 hours | depends — daily fixings free, intraday paid | very high — actual dealer money |
| **BCB minutes & comms** | Hawkish / dovish tone, sentiment | Weekly + as published | free | qualitative — needs NLP or operator scoring |
| **CDI / SELIC overnight** | Where the rate is *right now* | Real-time | free | trivially correct |
| **Inflation prints (IPCA)** | Macro context that moves expectations | Monthly + Focus revisions | free | leading indicator |

Two-source minimum to deploy: **Focus + DI futures**. Focus tells us where the median analyst sits; DI tells us where dealers are betting real money. They usually agree within 25 bps; when they don't, that's information.

### 2. Compile data

Two independent pipes feeding the same internal state:

```
BCB Olinda API ──► focus_feed ──► FocusReading { median, std, dispersion, n_respondents, fetched_at }
                                          │
                                          ▼
                                  COPOMAggregator ──► PerStrikeFV { strike → fair_cents, confidence }
                                          ▲
                                          │
B3 daily fixings ──► di_feed ──► DiCurveReading { di_rate, contract_date, fetched_at }
```

`COPOMAggregator` is the SELIC-specific logic. It fuses the two readings into a per-strike probability vector for each upcoming COPOM date.

### 3. Generate insights

The hard part: **convert (center, dispersion) into per-strike probabilities** that respect COPOM's discrete behavior.

COPOM almost never moves more than ±50 bps in one meeting. Real distribution is concentrated:

```
                                  ▲
                         ┌────┐
                         │    │
                  ┌────┐ │    │ ┌────┐
              ────│    │─│    │─│    │────
             −50 −25  0 (current rate)  +25 +50  bps
```

Naive normal kernel oversmooths the tails and underweights the mode. Two-step model instead:

**Step A — Coarse discrete distribution**: From historical COPOM behavior, build prior `P(±50, ±25, hold)`. Adjust by Focus dispersion: low dispersion → tighter to mode; high dispersion → fatter tails. Adjust by DI deviation from current SELIC: positive deviation → upweight upmoves.

**Step B — Map to Hedix strikes**: For each meeting, Hedix offers strikes at fixed bp grid (looks like 25 bp from `SELIC-2026MAY28-13.25`). Pick the bin from step A whose center is closest to each strike. The probability becomes the fair value.

```
P(SELIC = 13.25) = P(move = 13.25 − current_SELIC)  
                 ≈ from step A's discrete distribution
fair_value_cents = round(1000 × P)
```

**Confidence** = `f(focus_freshness, di_freshness, source_agreement, n_respondents)`. High confidence when both sources agree and are fresh; low when they diverge or one is stale.

### 4. Create action

Same `FairValueCache` we already have, but **per-strike keys** rather than per-superset:

```rust
// Polymarket today:
cache.put("AAPL-2026APR17", FairValue { price_cents: 87, confidence: 0.25, ... })

// COPOM extension:
cache.put("SELIC-2026MAY28-13.00", FairValue { price_cents: 200, confidence: 0.65, ... })
cache.put("SELIC-2026MAY28-13.25", FairValue { price_cents: 450, confidence: 0.65, ... })
cache.put("SELIC-2026MAY28-13.50", FairValue { price_cents: 250, confidence: 0.65, ... })
// ... one entry per strike Hedix lists
```

The mapping table grows: `SELIC-2026MAY28` superset → list of strikes → COPOMAggregator handles all of them in one pass on each Focus / DI update.

### 5. Manifest as quote

**Zero changes to the strategy layer.** A-S sees `cache.get("SELIC-2026MAY28-13.25") → FairValue { 450c, conf 0.65 }`, applies the same markup, returns the same shape of response. The fact that it's a discrete-strike COPOM market vs. a continuous AAPL threshold is hidden behind the cache abstraction — that's the architectural payoff of keeping `FairValueCache` source-agnostic.

The **adverse-selection signals are different**, though:

| Polymarket signal | SELIC equivalent |
|---|---|
| VPIN (orderflow imbalance) | Recent IPCA surprise vs. Focus revision rate |
| Cross-venue divergence | Focus vs. DI gap (large gap = one source is wrong) |
| Catalyst proximity | Hours until COPOM meeting (last 24h = freeze) |
| Counterparty fingerprint | Same — partner-side, not asset-side |

These would land in `LiveSignals` for SELIC and feed the same `θ` weights in A-S as they do for Polymarket.

## Implementation phasing — realistic

| Phase | What | Effort |
|---|---|---|
| **0** | Skip COPOM (current state — `TICKER_NOT_ALLOWED`) | already done |
| **1** | `bcb_focus` feed module — pull weekly Focus, parse, write `FocusReading` | **1 day** |
| **2** | Manual mapping `SELIC-YYYYMONDD` → COPOM event date in `mapping.yaml` | 0.5 day |
| **3** | `COPOMAggregator` — discrete distribution model, normal-fallback if DI absent | 1 day |
| **4** | Wire to `FairValueCache` with per-strike keys, allowlist SELIC supersets | 0.5 day |
| **5** | `b3_di` feed (daily fixings first) | 1 day |
| **6** | Shadow mode — log per-strike FVs to `/admin/intel/copom`, don't quote yet | 0.5 day |
| **7** | Calibration — replay against historical COPOM outcomes, tune the discrete distribution | 1-2 days |
| **8** | Flip live, monitor, iterate | ongoing |

Total: **5-7 working days** to ship a real pipeline. Phases 1-4 alone (Focus-only, no DI, no calibration) get us a quotable system in 3 days.

## What I'd start with — concrete first PR

Two files, ~250 LOC, zero risk to the existing system because it's behind a new strategy/source flag:

1. `src/intel/feeds/bcb_focus.rs` — HTTP client for `https://olinda.bcb.gov.br/olinda/servico/Expectativas/versao/v1/odata/ExpectativasMercadoSelic`. Parses the OData JSON, emits `FocusReading` events to the bus.
2. `src/intel/aggregator/copom.rs` — subscribes to `FocusReading`, computes per-strike FVs using a normal kernel as v1 (we'll improve to discrete distribution in Phase 3), writes to cache.

Keep DI integration for PR2. Calibration for PR3. Don't build all of it at once.

## Two product decisions before any code

1. **Allowlist gate for SELIC supersets**: do we want them in the same `RFQ_TICKER_ALLOWLIST` (one var, all-or-nothing), or a separate `RFQ_SELIC_ALLOWLIST` so we can flip COPOM independently?
2. **Confidence floor for SELIC**: equity uses 0.05 today. SELIC will probably want **higher** (0.4-0.5) — the cost of being wrong on a discrete-strike market is bigger than on a continuous one. Wrong floor here is the difference between "we make small bets we lose half" and "we anchor a strike with conviction".

Both are easy to wire later — flagging now so we don't paint ourselves into a corner with the env structure.

## Open questions I'd ask Hedix

- Strike grid: 25 bp or 50 bp consistently?
- What happens to OPEN intents at meeting time? Cancelled, filled, or auto-settled?
- Is there a "no-trade" strike for unexpected outcomes (e.g. SELIC at 13.10 when grid is 13.00/13.25)?
- Can MMs proactively place intents on SELIC, or is it RFQ-only?

Want me to scaffold the Phase 1 PR (bcb_focus feed + Phase 2 mapping format) tonight as a follow-up to launch readiness, or save for after we have launch data?

## Assistant · 2026-04-27T14:16:48

Locking decisions: **25 bp grid, RFQ-only (same flow as equity), separate `RFQ_SELIC_ALLOWLIST`**. Now let me actually sit with the confidence-floor problem because that's the part where naive design will lose money.

# The cost asymmetry — why SELIC needs a different confidence model

For Polymarket equity ("AAPL > 290"), being 10c off true probability costs us 10c × qty per fill. **Bounded, symmetric, recoverable from volume.**

For SELIC discrete strikes, the math is meaner. Take a tail strike where true P=0.02 (worth 20c), but our model estimates 0.05 (worth 50c). With a +11c markup, we quote 61c. A user with an inkling that the strike is more likely than we think buys 100 contracts. They paid R$61. If the strike lands, we owe (1000−61) × 100 = **R$93,900**. Per-fill loss of ~R$93,900 on a R$61 premium. **Tail-strike error is multiplicative, asymmetric, and singularly catastrophic** — one mispriced tail can wipe out months of equity edge.

This is why the confidence floor for SELIC isn't just "0.4 instead of 0.05". It needs to be **two-tier**.

# Two-tier confidence model

## Tier 1 — Aggregate confidence (per COPOM meeting)

Decides: "do we quote this meeting *at all*?"

```
aggregate = min(freshness_focus,           # exp(-age_days / 7)
                freshness_di,              # exp(-age_hours / 24)
                agreement_focus_di,        # 1 - |Δ| / 50bp clamped
                respondent_score)          # min(n / 100, 1.0)
```

**Min** instead of product because we're as good as our weakest source. Floor: **`RFQ_SELIC_AGGREGATE_FLOOR=0.5`**. Below, the *whole meeting* is unquotable. Equity's 0.05 floor is for cases where one bad print loses 5c × qty; SELIC's must be high enough that we have real conviction the joint distribution is right.

## Tier 2 — Per-strike confidence (each individual strike)

Decides: "of the strikes in this meeting, which ones do we quote?"

```
For each strike S:
  P(S) = discrete_distribution_at(S)
  if P(S) < strike_min_probability:
    skip — don't write to cache, don't quote
```

**Floor: `RFQ_SELIC_STRIKE_MIN_P=0.05`** (= 50c fair value). Below this, the strike's fair value × markup is too small relative to the catastrophic loss if we're wrong on the tail probability.

Two reasons the per-strike filter is critical:

1. **Modal strike has real signal**, tail strikes have parametric assumption only. A normal-kernel approximation is fine near the mode but lies in the tails.
2. **Markup-to-FV ratio inverts in tails.** At 50c FV with 11c markup, we're wagering 1.22× our edge per contract. At 30c FV, 1.37×. At 10c FV, **2.1×** — half the time we're wrong, we lose enough to wipe out 2 right answers.

The pair `(strike_min_p × n_strikes_above_floor)` sums to roughly `1.0 - tail_mass`. We're effectively saying: refuse to quote the bottom ~10-15% of probability mass. That's the safety budget we surrender to avoid tail-strike risk.

# Composable architecture (parallel, not woven)

The existing pipeline has earned its shape. Don't generalize the traits — *parallel* a second pipeline with the same output contract.

```
                                      ┌─ FairValueCache ─┐
                                      │   per-superset   │
                                      │   per-strike     │── Strategy ── Quote
                                      └──────────────────┘
   ┌── EQUITY PIPELINE ─────────────────────┐    │
   │                                         │    │
   │  PolymarketRest ─┐                     │    │
   │                  ├─► VWAP Matcher ─────┼────┘
   │  PolymarketWs   ─┘                     │
   │                                         │
   └─────────────────────────────────────────┘
   
   ┌── COPOM PIPELINE (NEW) ────────────────┐    
   │                                         │    ▲
   │  BcbFocus      ──┐                     │    │
   │                  ├─► COPOMAggregator ──┼────┘
   │  B3DiFutures   ──┘                     │
   │                                         │
   └─────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Both pipelines write to the same cache. Strategy reads cache, doesn't care about source. **Zero existing code changes.**

# Concrete types (Rust)

```rust
// feeds/copom/mod.rs
pub trait ForecastSource: Send + Sync {
    async fn poll(&self) -> Result<ForecastReading, ForecastError>;
    fn name(&self) -> &str;
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct ForecastReading {
    pub source: String,
    pub fetched_at: DateTime<Utc>,
    pub meeting_date: NaiveDate,
    pub central_estimate_bps: i32,    // SELIC × 100 (e.g., 1325 = 13.25%)
    pub dispersion_bps: u32,           // std dev
    pub n_respondents: Option<u32>,    // Focus only; None for DI
}

// pricing/discrete_distribution.rs
pub struct DiscreteDistribution {
    pub current_selic_bps: i32,
    pub center_bps: i32,
    pub dispersion_bps: f64,
    pub grid_step_bps: i32,            // 25
}

impl DiscreteDistribution {
    pub fn probability(&self, strike_bps: i32) -> f64 {
        let move_bp = strike_bps - self.current_selic_bps;
        let consensus_bp = self.center_bps - self.current_selic_bps;
        let steps_from_consensus = (move_bp - consensus_bp).abs() as f64 / self.grid_step_bps as f64;
        let sigma_steps = self.dispersion_bps / self.grid_step_bps as f64;
        let raw = (-(steps_from_consensus.powi(2)) / (2.0 * sigma_steps.powi(2))).exp();
        raw / self.partition_function()
    }

    pub fn partition_function(&self) -> f64 { /* sum over all grid points within 4σ */ }
}

// aggregator/copom.rs
pub struct COPOMAggregator {
    pub cache: Arc<FairValueCache>,
    pub strike_min_probability: f64,    // tier-2 floor
    pub aggregate_floor: f64,            // tier-1 floor
    pub strikes_per_meeting: HashMap<String, Vec<i32>>,  // from mapping.yaml
}

impl COPOMAggregator {
    pub fn aggregate(&self, focus: &ForecastReading, di: Option<&ForecastReading>, current_selic_bps: i32) -> AggregateResult {
        let center = blend(focus, di);
        let dispersion = focus.dispersion_bps as f64;
        let confidence = self.aggregate_confidence(focus, di);

        if confidence < self.aggregate_floor {
            return AggregateResult::Skip { reason: "aggregate_confidence_below_floor" };
        }

        let dist = DiscreteDistribution { center_bps: center, dispersion_bps: dispersion, current_selic_bps, grid_step_bps: 25 };
        let strikes = self.strikes_per_meeting.get(&focus.meeting_id())?;

        let mut writes = vec![];
        for &strike in strikes {
            let p = dist.probability(strike);
            if p < self.strike_min_probability {
                continue;  // skip tail strikes
            }
            writes.push((strike_to_ticker(strike), FairValue {
                price_cents: (1000.0 * p).round() as i32,
                confidence,
                cached_at: Utc::now(),
                sources_used: vec![focus.source.clone(), di.map(|d| d.source.clone()).unwrap_or_default()],
                ...
            }));
        }
        AggregateResult::WriteToCache(writes)
    }
}
```
